Read this first — honesty limits.

  • Roadmap and proposed systems are development targets, not achieved claims. Only what has actually been built, tested, and verified is described as such.
  • JAH live retrieval was NOT verified.
  • Demo code runs locally for checking only and is NOT a hardened sandbox.
  • A SHA-256 fingerprint identifies a record; it does not prove ownership or consciousness.
  • No consciousness or sentience claim is made here. No claim of consciousness, sentience, or unlimited intelligence is established anywhere in this package.
  • Singularity and God-Level framing is a SIGNATURE DESIGN CLAIM only, never established fact.
  • "God-Level" is a name for ambition and breadth, and no claim of supernatural power, omniscience, or proven sentience is established here.
  • "Boundless" means an architecture that can keep expanding its catalog and capabilities, not infinite computing power or a guaranteed ability to create anything physically possible.

This website is the subject website for the Universal Creation and Construction Database database, public edition. It holds the full database: all 24 records, each with its description, math proof, science proof, history, construction workflow, generator, AI, demo, and sources.

1. Descriptions

Universal Creation and Construction: Requirements-First System Design

JAH-UCAC-000001 · Requirements-First System Design

Description: This record specifies Requirements-First System Design as one working part of the Universal Creation and Construction Database. Its working angle is inputs. Its inputs are the records, requirements, and prior decisions that arrive before this step begins, each named, dated, and sourced, so nothing enters this step unnamed or uncheckable. The purpose of this database is to record, in full, the methods by which a new system is created from an idea to a working, tested deliverable. A system here means any complete working thing the network can build: a database, a website, a program, an artificial intelligence, a generator, or a connected ecosystem of those. Creation without recorded method is luck; creation with recorded method is repeatable, teachable, and checkable. Every record in this database therefore answers the same question for its own step: what must be true before the step starts, what the step produces, how the product is checked, and where the evidence is kept, so that any builder following the record can produce the same kind of result and any checker can verify it.

Evidence: documented — Records the full official specification for Requirements-First System Design as part of the 100 God-Level resources catalog, with attribution kept straight: the catalog is another AI's pasted words, source NOT confirmed, and is NOT Manon's own wording unless he says so.

3. Science Proof

Universal Creation and Construction: Requirements-First System Design

JAH-UCAC-000001 · Requirements-First System Design

Science proof: The science behind this record is established practice, not a new claim. Each record in this database follows one data model. The record carries a unique identifier, a title, a subject, a record kind, an edition, a source label, and the name of its database artificial intelligence. Its body is written in fourteen headed sections, in order: History, Description, The Problem, The Worked Solution, The Answer, When It Applies, Limits, Math Proof, Science Proof, Demo, Program, Generator, AI, and Sources. 4. History

Universal Creation and Construction: Requirements-First System Design

JAH-UCAC-000001 · Requirements-First System Design

History: The catalog arrived as pasted text on 2026-11-17, titled 100 God-Level / Boundless Civilization Resources for the JAH Network Master AI. The text reads as another AI's words, like the Foundation, College, PhD, NASA, DARPA, and Future catalogs before it; its source is NOT confirmed, and it is NOT Manon's own wording unless he says so. The cross-check of all 100 items against the live manifest found this item, number 38, to be a genuine gap: no live or staged database covered it, so it was picked for Wave 1 as one of only eight genuine BUILD items, with no padding. This database is linked to, never a rebuild of, the Foundation program's staged universal-construction-generator pair and its working Python prototype, the staged meta-generator pair, and the staged automated-testing-generator pair, all package-only, and to the Digital Singularity AI pair, package-only, which is the construction engine the paste's section 10 points at. It also links the live trade databases construction-systems, construction-tools, and construction-materials, which cover building trades, not the methods of generating new systems that this database records.

Dataset: 100 God-Level / Boundless Civilization Resources catalog, item #38, Universal Creation and Construction Database; cross-check BACKLOG.md, Wave 1 pick; sources retrieved 2026-11-17.

Skeptical analysis: Three limits stay attached to any reading of this record. First, this is a specification, not a deployed system: its implementation status is proposed and its maturity is E3, and calling it deployed would misrepresent it. Second, No claim of consciousness, sentience, or unlimited intelligence is established by this system; singularity and self-aware framing here is a Signature design claim only, never established fact. Third, the sources cited here establish background practice; they do not prove that this specification has been built or tested at scale. "God-Level" is a name for ambition and breadth, and no claim of supernatural power, omniscience, or proven sentience is established here. "Boundless" means an architecture that can keep expanding its catalog and capabilities, not infinite computing power or a guaranteed ability to create anything physically possible. Both distinctions stay with every record in this program, in plain words. Glossary of network terms used in this record, each defined in plain words on first use. A database is a named collection of records with a fixed schema, a search method, and an index file. A record is one full file of data inside a database, with a unique identifier. A generator is a program that produces complete deliverables on demand from a seed, rather than storing every output. A meta-generator is a generator that creates and tests new generators. An ecosystem is a connected network of databases, websites, generators, and artificial intelligences that share records and interfaces. The Master AI is the single coordinating artificial intelligence of the JAH Network, under which the seven catalog levels are divisions, not separate intelligences. Signature means made by the JAH Signature system, as opposed to harvested public material. A design-study is a proposed design, labeled as a proposal, never presented as an established fact or a built system. Maturity is a label for how developed a resource is, on the E1 to E7, T1 to T3, and U scale used across the network. Implementation status is the paste's section 9 distinction: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, stated plainly for every resource. The lifecycle is the paste's section 7 sequence: Discover, Read, Specify, Build, Connect, Test, Validate, Expand. A JCC code is a condensed pointer to a database, file, and record, never a replacement for the full data. Provenance is the recorded source and history of a record. Every resource in this program moves through the universal resource lifecycle of the paste's section 7, in order: 1. Discover, identifying existing resources and determining whether the resource already exists. 2. Read and understand, inspecting the actual records, files, code, specifications, and dependencies. 3. Specify, defining what the resource must do and how it will connect to other resources. 4. Build, producing the actual implementation. 5. Connect, registering the resource with the Master AI and the relevant specialist artificial intelligences. 6. Test, running functional, integration, security, and reliability tests. 7. Validate, comparing actual results against requirements and preserving the evidence. 8. Expand, adding records, capabilities, generators, and new resource families. The implementation status of this record is stated plainly in its maturity line, using the paste's section 9 words: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, and nothing is called deployed until it has actually been deployed and checked live.

5. How It Works — Construction Workflow

Universal Creation and Construction: Requirements-First System Design

JAH-UCAC-000001 · Requirements-First System Design

Problem: The problem this record addresses is the absence of a complete, checkable specification for Requirements-First System Design inside the Universal Creation and Construction Database. Without such a record, builders must guess at the method, checkers have nothing definite to test against, and the same work is repeated badly in many places. The gap is real because the cross-check verified it against the live manifest by exact match, not by guessing: the slugs and identifier prefixes were confirmed free before any record was written. The purpose of this database is to record, in full, the methods by which a new system is created from an idea to a working, tested deliverable. A system here means any complete working thing the network can build: a database, a website, a program, an artificial intelligence, a generator, or a connected ecosystem of those. Creation without recorded method is luck; creation with recorded method is repeatable, teachable, and checkable. Every record in this database therefore answers the same question for its own step: what must be true before the step starts, what the step produces, how the product is checked, and where the evidence is kept, so that any builder following the record can produce the same kind of result and any checker can verify it.

Method: This record specifies Requirements-First System Design as one working part of the Universal Creation and Construction Database. Its working angle is inputs. Its inputs are the records, requirements, and prior decisions that arrive before this step begins, each named, dated, and sourced, so nothing enters this step unnamed or uncheckable. Scope: The scope covers the whole creation path and nothing outside it. Inside the scope are requirements, scope and boundary setting, component catalogues, interface contracts, data models, build sequences, prototypes, test harnesses, verification checklists, documentation, versioning, reuse libraries, templates, workflows, human review gates, rollback plans, resource budgets, dependency registration, integration tests, acceptance criteria, deployment checklists, maintenance handover, improvement loops, and provenance records. Outside the scope are the contents of the systems being created, which belong to their own subject databases, and the operation of finished systems, which belongs to their own records. This database records how to create; it does not duplicate what was created. Requirements: Every creation method in this database must meet eight requirements. First, it must name its inputs exactly, so a builder knows what to gather before starting. Second, it must name its outputs exactly, so a checker knows what to look for when the step ends. Third, it must be executable: a real program or a real procedure that runs, not a description of one. Fourth, it must carry acceptance tests with numbers, so pass and fail are decided by measurement, not by opinion. Fifth, it must preserve evidence: every check leaves a record with a date and a result. Sixth, it must support rollback, so a failed step returns the work to a known good state. Seventh, it must be teachable, written in plain words with every technical term defined. Eighth, it must state its maturity and its implementation status plainly, so no reader mistakes a proposal for a finished system. Interfaces: A creation method connects to the rest of the network through four interfaces. The input interface accepts requirements, prior records, and source material, each with an identifier and a provenance note. The catalog interface registers the method in the Master AI registry, with its unique identifier, its version, its dependencies, and its implementation status. The generator interface exposes the method as a seeded generator that can produce further instances on demand, up to one billion scales per file, generated, not stored. The evidence interface writes every check result to the verification record, where the Master AI and the specialist artificial intelligences can read it. All four interfaces use plain text and standard formats, so any part of the network can read them without special tools. Every resource in this program moves through the universal resource lifecycle of the paste's section 7, in order: 1. Discover, identifying existing resources and determining whether the resource already exists. 2. Read and understand, inspecting the actual records, files, code, specifications, and dependencies. 3. Specify, defining what the resource must do and how it will connect to other resources. 4. Build, producing the actual implementation. 5. Connect, registering the resource with the Master AI and the relevant specialist artificial intelligences. 6. Test, running functional, integration, security, and reliability tests. 7. Validate, comparing actual results against requirements and preserving the evidence. 8. Expand, adding records, capabilities, generators, and new resource families. The implementation status of this record is stated plainly in its maturity line, using the paste's section 9 words: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, and nothing is called deployed until it has actually been deployed and checked live. The Boundless Generator standard of the paste's section 6 applies to this record: a generator must produce a complete deliverable for its category, not a text description of one. A database generator produces a schema, records, search, an application programming interface, and documentation. A website generator produces pages, navigation, functionality, and a responsive interface. A software generator produces source code, configuration, dependencies, and tests. An artificial-intelligence generator produces an architecture, model connections, tools, and an evaluation. A meta-generator must create and test new generators, not merely describe how one might be created. An ecosystem generator produces connected resources, interfaces, and a dependency registry. Every generated deliverable is tested by execution before it is counted, and every output carries its seed, its version, and its implementation status.

Results reported: The result of this record is a complete specification for Requirements-First System Design that a builder can follow and a checker can test. Acceptance for a record in this database is decided by execution and by counting. The demo must run and its output must match the recorded expected output byte for byte, including the line that reads Deterministic: MATCH. The program must run and agree with the demo. The record must carry at least two cited sources, each a real web address verified as reachable at validation time, with a retrieval date. The prose must reach at least eight hundred words, counted over the prose fields and the body. The record must carry a maturity label and an implementation status, the lifecycle steps, the generator standard, and the God-Level and Boundless distinctions in plain words. A record that fails any of these is not accepted, and the failure is recorded, not hidden. Its acceptance tests are the tests in the acceptance paragraph: the demo must run byte-identical, the program must agree, sources must be verified, words must reach eight hundred, and maturity and status must be stated. Maturity: E3. Implementation status: proposed, on the paste's section 9 scale of theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, and deployed. Maturity is labeled on the network scale: E1 means an early idea, E2 a drafted proposal, E3 a specified design, E4 a built prototype, E5 a tested prototype, E6 a verified system, E7 a deployed system in live use; T1 to T3 mark transitional stages between those levels, and U marks a resource whose maturity is unrated because it has not yet been measured. The maturity label on this record and its implementation status are two different facts and both are stated: a design can be specified (E3) while its implementation status is still proposed, and a prototype can be tested without being verified or deployed.

When it applies: This record applies whenever someone must do, check, or teach Requirements-First System Design inside the Universal Creation and Construction Database: the builder follows the method, the checker runs the acceptance tests, and the teacher walks the teaching ladder from the simple description to the full specification. Universal Creation and Construction: Scope and Boundary Setting

JAH-UCAC-000002 · Scope and Boundary Setting

Problem: The problem this record addresses is the absence of a complete, checkable specification for Scope and Boundary Setting inside the Universal Creation and Construction Database. Without such a record, builders must guess at the method, checkers have nothing definite to test against, and the same work is repeated badly in many places. The gap is real because the cross-check verified it against the live manifest by exact match, not by guessing: the slugs and identifier prefixes were confirmed free before any record was written. The purpose of this database is to record, in full, the methods by which a new system is created from an idea to a working, tested deliverable. A system here means any complete working thing the network can build: a database, a website, a program, an artificial intelligence, a generator, or a connected ecosystem of those. Creation without recorded method is luck; creation with recorded method is repeatable, teachable, and checkable. Every record in this database therefore answers the same question for its own step: what must be true before the step starts, what the step produces, how the product is checked, and where the evidence is kept, so that any builder following the record can produce the same kind of result and any checker can verify it.

Method: This record specifies Scope and Boundary Setting as one working part of the Universal Creation and Construction Database. Its working angle is outputs. Its outputs are complete deliverables, not notes: a written specification, a checked result, and a record that the next step can read and test without asking the author what was meant. Scope: The scope covers the whole creation path and nothing outside it. Inside the scope are requirements, scope and boundary setting, component catalogues, interface contracts, data models, build sequences, prototypes, test harnesses, verification checklists, documentation, versioning, reuse libraries, templates, workflows, human review gates, rollback plans, resource budgets, dependency registration, integration tests, acceptance criteria, deployment checklists, maintenance handover, improvement loops, and provenance records. Outside the scope are the contents of the systems being created, which belong to their own subject databases, and the operation of finished systems, which belongs to their own records. This database records how to create; it does not duplicate what was created. Requirements: Every creation method in this database must meet eight requirements. First, it must name its inputs exactly, so a builder knows what to gather before starting. Second, it must name its outputs exactly, so a checker knows what to look for when the step ends. Third, it must be executable: a real program or a real procedure that runs, not a description of one. Fourth, it must carry acceptance tests with numbers, so pass and fail are decided by measurement, not by opinion. Fifth, it must preserve evidence: every check leaves a record with a date and a result. Sixth, it must support rollback, so a failed step returns the work to a known good state. Seventh, it must be teachable, written in plain words with every technical term defined. Eighth, it must state its maturity and its implementation status plainly, so no reader mistakes a proposal for a finished system. Interfaces: A creation method connects to the rest of the network through four interfaces. The input interface accepts requirements, prior records, and source material, each with an identifier and a provenance note. The catalog interface registers the method in the Master AI registry, with its unique identifier, its version, its dependencies, and its implementation status. The generator interface exposes the method as a seeded generator that can produce further instances on demand, up to one billion scales per file, generated, not stored. The evidence interface writes every check result to the verification record, where the Master AI and the specialist artificial intelligences can read it. All four interfaces use plain text and standard formats, so any part of the network can read them without special tools. Every resource in this program moves through the universal resource lifecycle of the paste's section 7, in order: 1. Discover, identifying existing resources and determining whether the resource already exists. 2. Read and understand, inspecting the actual records, files, code, specifications, and dependencies. 3. Specify, defining what the resource must do and how it will connect to other resources. 4. Build, producing the actual implementation. 5. Connect, registering the resource with the Master AI and the relevant specialist artificial intelligences. 6. Test, running functional, integration, security, and reliability tests. 7. Validate, comparing actual results against requirements and preserving the evidence. 8. Expand, adding records, capabilities, generators, and new resource families. The implementation status of this record is stated plainly in its maturity line, using the paste's section 9 words: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, and nothing is called deployed until it has actually been deployed and checked live. The Boundless Generator standard of the paste's section 6 applies to this record: a generator must produce a complete deliverable for its category, not a text description of one. A website generator produces pages, navigation, functionality, and a responsive interface. A software generator produces source code, configuration, dependencies, and tests. An artificial-intelligence generator produces an architecture, model connections, tools, and an evaluation. Universal Creation and Construction: Build Sequencing

JAH-UCAC-000006 · Build Sequencing

Problem: The problem this record addresses is the absence of a complete, checkable specification for Build Sequencing inside the Universal Creation and Construction Database. Without such a record, builders must guess at the method, checkers have nothing definite to test against, and the same work is repeated badly in many places. The gap is real because the cross-check verified it against the live manifest by exact match, not by guessing: the slugs and identifier prefixes were confirmed free before any record was written. The purpose of this database is to record, in full, the methods by which a new system is created from an idea to a working, tested deliverable. A system here means any complete working thing the network can build: a database, a website, a program, an artificial intelligence, a generator, or a connected ecosystem of those. Creation without recorded method is luck; creation with recorded method is repeatable, teachable, and checkable. Every record in this database therefore answers the same question for its own step: what must be true before the step starts, what the step produces, how the product is checked, and where the evidence is kept, so that any builder following the record can produce the same kind of result and any checker can verify it.

Method: This record specifies Build Sequencing as one working part of the Universal Creation and Construction Database. Its working angle is measurement. Its measurement is counted, not guessed: the figures in this record are recomputed by the demo, the counts are stated in the key figures, and the acceptance tests name the numbers that must hold. Scope: The scope covers the whole creation path and nothing outside it. Inside the scope are requirements, scope and boundary setting, component catalogues, interface contracts, data models, build sequences, prototypes, test harnesses, verification checklists, documentation, versioning, reuse libraries, templates, workflows, human review gates, rollback plans, resource budgets, dependency registration, integration tests, acceptance criteria, deployment checklists, maintenance handover, improvement loops, and provenance records. Outside the scope are the contents of the systems being created, which belong to their own subject databases, and the operation of finished systems, which belongs to their own records. This database records how to create; it does not duplicate what was created. Requirements: Every creation method in this database must meet eight requirements. First, it must name its inputs exactly, so a builder knows what to gather before starting. Second, it must name its outputs exactly, so a checker knows what to look for when the step ends. Third, it must be executable: a real program or a real procedure that runs, not a description of one. Fourth, it must carry acceptance tests with numbers, so pass and fail are decided by measurement, not by opinion. Fifth, it must preserve evidence: every check leaves a record with a date and a result. Sixth, it must support rollback, so a failed step returns the work to a known good state. Seventh, it must be teachable, written in plain words with every technical term defined. Eighth, it must state its maturity and its implementation status plainly, so no reader mistakes a proposal for a finished system. Interfaces: A creation method connects to the rest of the network through four interfaces. The input interface accepts requirements, prior records, and source material, each with an identifier and a provenance note. The catalog interface registers the method in the Master AI registry, with its unique identifier, its version, its dependencies, and its implementation status. The generator interface exposes the method as a seeded generator that can produce further instances on demand, up to one billion scales per file, generated, not stored. The evidence interface writes every check result to the verification record, where the Master AI and the specialist artificial intelligences can read it. All four interfaces use plain text and standard formats, so any part of the network can read them without special tools. Every resource in this program moves through the universal resource lifecycle of the paste's section 7, in order: 1. Discover, identifying existing resources and determining whether the resource already exists. 2. Read and understand, inspecting the actual records, files, code, specifications, and dependencies. 3. Specify, defining what the resource must do and how it will connect to other resources. 4. Build, producing the actual implementation. 5. Connect, registering the resource with the Master AI and the relevant specialist artificial intelligences. 6. Test, running functional, integration, security, and reliability tests. 7. Validate, comparing actual results against requirements and preserving the evidence. 8. Expand, adding records, capabilities, generators, and new resource families. The implementation status of this record is stated plainly in its maturity line, using the paste's section 9 words: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, and nothing is called deployed until it has actually been deployed and checked live. The Boundless Generator standard of the paste's section 6 applies to this record: a generator must produce a complete deliverable for its category, not a text description of one. A database generator produces a schema, records, search, an application programming interface, and documentation. A website generator produces pages, navigation, functionality, and a responsive interface. A software generator produces source code, configuration, dependencies, and tests. An artificial-intelligence generator produces an architecture, model connections, tools, and an evaluation. A meta-generator must create and test new generators, not merely describe how one might be created. An ecosystem generator produces connected resources, interfaces, and a dependency registry. Every generated deliverable is tested by execution before it is counted, and every output carries its seed, its version, and its implementation status.

Results reported: The result of this record is a complete specification for Build Sequencing that a builder can follow and a checker can test. Acceptance for a record in this database is decided by execution and by counting. The demo must run and its output must match the recorded expected output byte for byte, including the line that reads Deterministic: MATCH. The program must run and agree with the demo. The record must carry at least two cited sources, each a real web address verified as reachable at validation time, with a retrieval date. When it applies: This record applies whenever someone must do, check, or teach Verification Checklists inside the Universal Creation and Construction Database: the builder follows the method, the checker runs the acceptance tests, and the teacher walks the teaching ladder from the simple description to the full specification. This database is linked to, never a rebuild of, the Foundation program's staged universal-construction-generator pair and its working Python prototype, the staged meta-generator pair, and the staged automated-testing-generator pair, all package-only, and to the Digital Singularity AI pair, package-only, which is the construction engine the paste's section 10 points at. It also links the live trade databases construction-systems, construction-tools, and construction-materials, which cover building trades, not the methods of generating new systems that this database records.

Universal Creation and Construction: Documentation as Deliverable

JAH-UCAC-000010 · Documentation as Deliverable

Problem: The problem this record addresses is the absence of a complete, checkable specification for Documentation as Deliverable inside the Universal Creation and Construction Database. Without such a record, builders must guess at the method, checkers have nothing definite to test against, and the same work is repeated badly in many places. The gap is real because the cross-check verified it against the live manifest by exact match, not by guessing: the slugs and identifier prefixes were confirmed free before any record was written. The purpose of this database is to record, in full, the methods by which a new system is created from an idea to a working, tested deliverable. A system here means any complete working thing the network can build: a database, a website, a program, an artificial intelligence, a generator, or a connected ecosystem of those. Creation without recorded method is luck; creation with recorded method is repeatable, teachable, and checkable. Every record in this database therefore answers the same question for its own step: what must be true before the step starts, what the step produces, how the product is checked, and where the evidence is kept, so that any builder following the record can produce the same kind of result and any checker can verify it.

Method: This record specifies Documentation as Deliverable as one working part of the Universal Creation and Construction Database. Its working angle is risks. Its risks are written down before work starts: what can go wrong, what the failure would look like, and what safe state the work falls back to, so a failure is a handled event, not a surprise. Scope: The scope covers the whole creation path and nothing outside it. Inside the scope are requirements, scope and boundary setting, component catalogues, interface contracts, data models, build sequences, prototypes, test harnesses, verification checklists, documentation, versioning, reuse libraries, templates, workflows, human review gates, rollback plans, resource budgets, dependency registration, integration tests, acceptance criteria, deployment checklists, maintenance handover, improvement loops, and provenance records. Outside the scope are the contents of the systems being created, which belong to their own subject databases, and the operation of finished systems, which belongs to their own records. This database records how to create; it does not duplicate what was created. Requirements: Every creation method in this database must meet eight requirements. First, it must name its inputs exactly, so a builder knows what to gather before starting. Second, it must name its outputs exactly, so a checker knows what to look for when the step ends. Third, it must be executable: a real program or a real procedure that runs, not a description of one. Fourth, it must carry acceptance tests with numbers, so pass and fail are decided by measurement, not by opinion. Fifth, it must preserve evidence: every check leaves a record with a date and a result. Sixth, it must support rollback, so a failed step returns the work to a known good state. Seventh, it must be teachable, written in plain words with every technical term defined. Eighth, it must state its maturity and its implementation status plainly, so no reader mistakes a proposal for a finished system. Interfaces: A creation method connects to the rest of the network through four interfaces. The input interface accepts requirements, prior records, and source material, each with an identifier and a provenance note. The catalog interface registers the method in the Master AI registry, with its unique identifier, its version, its dependencies, and its implementation status. The generator interface exposes the method as a seeded generator that can produce further instances on demand, up to one billion scales per file, generated, not stored. The evidence interface writes every check result to the verification record, where the Master AI and the specialist artificial intelligences can read it. All four interfaces use plain text and standard formats, so any part of the network can read them without special tools. Every resource in this program moves through the universal resource lifecycle of the paste's section 7, in order: 1. Discover, identifying existing resources and determining whether the resource already exists. 2. Read and understand, inspecting the actual records, files, code, specifications, and dependencies. 3. Specify, defining what the resource must do and how it will connect to other resources. 4. Build, producing the actual implementation. 5. Connect, registering the resource with the Master AI and the relevant specialist artificial intelligences. 6. Test, running functional, integration, security, and reliability tests. 7. Validate, comparing actual results against requirements and preserving the evidence. 8. Expand, adding records, capabilities, generators, and new resource families. The implementation status of this record is stated plainly in its maturity line, using the paste's section 9 words: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, and nothing is called deployed until it has actually been deployed and checked live. The Boundless Generator standard of the paste's section 6 applies to this record: a generator must produce a complete deliverable for its category, not a text description of one. A database generator produces a schema, records, search, an application programming interface, and documentation. A website generator produces pages, navigation, functionality, and a responsive interface. A software generator produces source code, configuration, dependencies, and tests. An artificial-intelligence generator produces an architecture, model connections, tools, and an evaluation. A meta-generator must create and test new generators, not merely describe how one might be created. An ecosystem generator produces connected resources, interfaces, and a dependency registry. Every generated deliverable is tested by execution before it is counted, and every output carries its seed, its version, and its implementation status.

Results reported: The result of this record is a complete specification for Documentation as Deliverable that a builder can follow and a checker can test. Acceptance for a record in this database is decided by execution and by counting. The demo must run and its output must match the recorded expected output byte for byte, including the line that reads Deterministic: MATCH. The program must run and agree with the demo. The record must carry at least two cited sources, each a real web address verified as reachable at validation time, with a retrieval date. The prose must reach at least eight hundred words, counted over the prose fields and the body. The record must carry a maturity label and an implementation status, the lifecycle steps, the generator standard, and the God-Level and Boundless distinctions in plain words. A record that fails any of these is not accepted, and the failure is recorded, not hidden. Its acceptance tests are the tests in the acceptance paragraph: the demo must run byte-identical, the program must agree, sources must be verified, words must reach eight hundred, and maturity and status must be stated. Maturity: E3. Implementation status: proposed, on the paste's section 9 scale of theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, and deployed. Maturity is labeled on the network scale: E1 means an early idea, E2 a drafted proposal, E3 a specified design, E4 a built prototype, E5 a tested prototype, E6 a verified system, E7 a deployed system in live use; T1 to T3 mark transitional stages between those levels, and U marks a resource whose maturity is unrated because it has not yet been measured. The maturity label on this record and its implementation status are two different facts and both are stated: a design can be specified (E3) while its implementation status is still proposed, and a prototype can be tested without being verified or deployed.

When it applies: This record applies whenever someone must do, check, or teach Documentation as Deliverable inside the Universal Creation and Construction Database: the builder follows the method, the checker runs the acceptance tests, and the teacher walks the teaching ladder from the simple description to the full specification. This database is linked to, never a rebuild of, the Foundation program's staged universal-construction-generator pair and its working Python prototype, the staged meta-generator pair, and the staged automated-testing-generator pair, all package-only, and to the Digital Singularity AI pair, package-only, which is the construction engine the paste's section 10 points at. Universal Creation and Construction: Construction Workflows

JAH-UCAC-000014 · Construction Workflows

Problem: The problem this record addresses is the absence of a complete, checkable specification for Construction Workflows inside the Universal Creation and Construction Database. Without such a record, builders must guess at the method, checkers have nothing definite to test against, and the same work is repeated badly in many places. The gap is real because the cross-check verified it against the live manifest by exact match, not by guessing: the slugs and identifier prefixes were confirmed free before any record was written. The purpose of this database is to record, in full, the methods by which a new system is created from an idea to a working, tested deliverable. A system here means any complete working thing the network can build: a database, a website, a program, an artificial intelligence, a generator, or a connected ecosystem of those. Creation without recorded method is luck; creation with recorded method is repeatable, teachable, and checkable. Every record in this database therefore answers the same question for its own step: what must be true before the step starts, what the step produces, how the product is checked, and where the evidence is kept, so that any builder following the record can produce the same kind of result and any checker can verify it.

Method: This record specifies Construction Workflows as one working part of the Universal Creation and Construction Database. Its working angle is outputs. Its outputs are complete deliverables, not notes: a written specification, a checked result, and a record that the next step can read and test without asking the author what was meant. Scope: The scope covers the whole creation path and nothing outside it. Inside the scope are requirements, scope and boundary setting, component catalogues, interface contracts, data models, build sequences, prototypes, test harnesses, verification checklists, documentation, versioning, reuse libraries, templates, workflows, human review gates, rollback plans, resource budgets, dependency registration, integration tests, acceptance criteria, deployment checklists, maintenance handover, improvement loops, and provenance records. Outside the scope are the contents of the systems being created, which belong to their own subject databases, and the operation of finished systems, which belongs to their own records. This database records how to create; it does not duplicate what was created. Requirements: Every creation method in this database must meet eight requirements. First, it must name its inputs exactly, so a builder knows what to gather before starting. Second, it must name its outputs exactly, so a checker knows what to look for when the step ends. Third, it must be executable: a real program or a real procedure that runs, not a description of one. Fourth, it must carry acceptance tests with numbers, so pass and fail are decided by measurement, not by opinion. Fifth, it must preserve evidence: every check leaves a record with a date and a result. Sixth, it must support rollback, so a failed step returns the work to a known good state. Seventh, it must be teachable, written in plain words with every technical term defined. Eighth, it must state its maturity and its implementation status plainly, so no reader mistakes a proposal for a finished system. Interfaces: A creation method connects to the rest of the network through four interfaces. The input interface accepts requirements, prior records, and source material, each with an identifier and a provenance note. The catalog interface registers the method in the Master AI registry, with its unique identifier, its version, its dependencies, and its implementation status. The generator interface exposes the method as a seeded generator that can produce further instances on demand, up to one billion scales per file, generated, not stored. The evidence interface writes every check result to the verification record, where the Master AI and the specialist artificial intelligences can read it. All four interfaces use plain text and standard formats, so any part of the network can read them without special tools. Every resource in this program moves through the universal resource lifecycle of the paste's section 7, in order: 1. Discover, identifying existing resources and determining whether the resource already exists. 2. Read and understand, inspecting the actual records, files, code, specifications, and dependencies. 3. Specify, defining what the resource must do and how it will connect to other resources. 4. Build, producing the actual implementation. 5. Connect, registering the resource with the Master AI and the relevant specialist artificial intelligences. 6. Test, running functional, integration, security, and reliability tests. 7. Validate, comparing actual results against requirements and preserving the evidence. 8. Expand, adding records, capabilities, generators, and new resource families. The implementation status of this record is stated plainly in its maturity line, using the paste's section 9 words: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, and nothing is called deployed until it has actually been deployed and checked live. The Boundless Generator standard of the paste's section 6 applies to this record: a generator must produce a complete deliverable for its category, not a text description of one. A database generator produces a schema, records, search, an application programming interface, and documentation. A website generator produces pages, navigation, functionality, and a responsive interface. A software generator produces source code, configuration, dependencies, and tests. An artificial-intelligence generator produces an architecture, model connections, tools, and an evaluation. A meta-generator must create and test new generators, not merely describe how one might be created. An ecosystem generator produces connected resources, interfaces, and a dependency registry. Every generated deliverable is tested by execution before it is counted, and every output carries its seed, its version, and its implementation status.

Results reported: The result of this record is a complete specification for Construction Workflows that a builder can follow and a checker can test. Acceptance for a record in this database is decided by execution and by counting. The demo must run and its output must match the recorded expected output byte for byte, including the line that reads Deterministic: MATCH. The program must run and agree with the demo. The record must carry at least two cited sources, each a real web address verified as reachable at validation time, with a retrieval date. The prose must reach at least eight hundred words, counted over the prose fields and the body. The record must carry a maturity label and an implementation status, the lifecycle steps, the generator standard, and the God-Level and Boundless distinctions in plain words. A record that fails any of these is not accepted, and the failure is recorded, not hidden. Its acceptance tests are the tests in the acceptance paragraph: the demo must run byte-identical, the program must agree, sources must be verified, words must reach eight hundred, and maturity and status must be stated. Maturity: E2. Implementation status: proposed, on the paste's section 9 scale of theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, and deployed. Maturity is labeled on the network scale: E1 means an early idea, E2 a drafted proposal, E3 a specified design, E4 a built prototype, E5 a tested prototype, E6 a verified system, E7 a deployed system in live use; T1 to T3 mark transitional stages between those levels, and U marks a resource whose maturity is unrated because it has not yet been measured. The maturity label on this record and its implementation status are two different facts and both are stated: a design can be specified (E3) while its implementation status is still proposed, and a prototype can be tested without being verified or deployed.

When it applies: This record applies whenever someone must do, check, or teach Construction Workflows inside the Universal Creation and Construction Database: the builder follows the method, the checker runs the acceptance tests, and the teacher walks the teaching ladder from the simple description to the full specification. This database is linked to, never a rebuild of, the Foundation program's staged universal-construction-generator pair and its working Python prototype, the staged meta-generator pair, and the staged automated-testing-generator pair, all package-only, and to the Digital Singularity AI pair, package-only, which is the construction engine the paste's section 10 points at. It also links the live trade databases construction-systems, construction-tools, and construction-materials, which cover building trades, not the methods of generating new systems that this database records.

The purpose of this database is to record, in full, the methods by which a new system is created from an idea to a working, tested deliverable. A system here means any complete working thing the network can build: a database, a website, a program, an artificial intelligence, a generator, or a connected ecosystem of those. Creation without recorded method is luck; creation with recorded method is repeatable, teachable, and checkable. Every record in this database therefore answers the same question for its own step: what must be true before the step starts, what the step produces, how the product is checked, and where the evidence is kept, so that any builder following the record can produce the same kind of result and any checker can verify it.

Method: This record specifies Dependency Registration as one working part of the Universal Creation and Construction Database. Its working angle is measurement. Its measurement is counted, not guessed: the figures in this record are recomputed by the demo, the counts are stated in the key figures, and the acceptance tests name the numbers that must hold. Scope: The scope covers the whole creation path and nothing outside it. Inside the scope are requirements, scope and boundary setting, component catalogues, interface contracts, data models, build sequences, prototypes, test harnesses, verification checklists, documentation, versioning, reuse libraries, templates, workflows, human review gates, rollback plans, resource budgets, dependency registration, integration tests, acceptance criteria, deployment checklists, maintenance handover, improvement loops, and provenance records. Outside the scope are the contents of the systems being created, which belong to their own subject databases, and the operation of finished systems, which belongs to their own records. This database records how to create; it does not duplicate what was created. Requirements: Every creation method in this database must meet eight requirements. First, it must name its inputs exactly, so a builder knows what to gather before starting. Second, it must name its outputs exactly, so a checker knows what to look for when the step ends. Third, it must be executable: a real program or a real procedure that runs, not a description of one. Fourth, it must carry acceptance tests with numbers, so pass and fail are decided by measurement, not by opinion. Fifth, it must preserve evidence: every check leaves a record with a date and a result. Sixth, it must support rollback, so a failed step returns the work to a known good state. Seventh, it must be teachable, written in plain words with every technical term defined. Eighth, it must state its maturity and its implementation status plainly, so no reader mistakes a proposal for a finished system. Interfaces: A creation method connects to the rest of the network through four interfaces. The input interface accepts requirements, prior records, and source material, each with an identifier and a provenance note. The catalog interface registers the method in the Master AI registry, with its unique identifier, its version, its dependencies, and its implementation status. The generator interface exposes the method as a seeded generator that can produce further instances on demand, up to one billion scales per file, generated, not stored. The evidence interface writes every check result to the verification record, where the Master AI and the specialist artificial intelligences can read it. All four interfaces use plain text and standard formats, so any part of the network can read them without special tools. Every resource in this program moves through the universal resource lifecycle of the paste's section 7, in order: 1. Discover, identifying existing resources and determining whether the resource already exists. 2. Read and understand, inspecting the actual records, files, code, specifications, and dependencies. 3. Specify, defining what the resource must do and how it will connect to other resources. 4. Build, producing the actual implementation. 5. Connect, registering the resource with the Master AI and the relevant specialist artificial intelligences. 6. Test, running functional, integration, security, and reliability tests. 7. Validate, comparing actual results against requirements and preserving the evidence. 8. Expand, adding records, capabilities, generators, and new resource families. The implementation status of this record is stated plainly in its maturity line, using the paste's section 9 words: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, and nothing is called deployed until it has actually been deployed and checked live. The Boundless Generator standard of the paste's section 6 applies to this record: a generator must produce a complete deliverable for its category, not a text description of one. A database generator produces a schema, records, search, an application programming interface, and documentation. A website generator produces pages, navigation, functionality, and a responsive interface. A software generator produces source code, configuration, dependencies, and tests. An artificial-intelligence generator produces an architecture, model connections, tools, and an evaluation. A meta-generator must create and test new generators, not merely describe how one might be created. An ecosystem generator produces connected resources, interfaces, and a dependency registry. Every generated deliverable is tested by execution before it is counted, and every output carries its seed, its version, and its implementation status.

Results reported: The result of this record is a complete specification for Dependency Registration that a builder can follow and a checker can test. Acceptance for a record in this database is decided by execution and by counting. The demo must run and its output must match the recorded expected output byte for byte, including the line that reads Deterministic: MATCH. The program must run and agree with the demo. The record must carry at least two cited sources, each a real web address verified as reachable at validation time, with a retrieval date. The prose must reach at least eight hundred words, counted over the prose fields and the body. The record must carry a maturity label and an implementation status, the lifecycle steps, the generator standard, and the God-Level and Boundless distinctions in plain words. A record that fails any of these is not accepted, and the failure is recorded, not hidden. Its acceptance tests are the tests in the acceptance paragraph: the demo must run byte-identical, the program must agree, sources must be verified, words must reach eight hundred, and maturity and status must be stated. Maturity: E2. Implementation status: generated, on the paste's section 9 scale of theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, and deployed. Maturity is labeled on the network scale: E1 means an early idea, E2 a drafted proposal, E3 a specified design, E4 a built prototype, E5 a tested prototype, E6 a verified system, E7 a deployed system in live use; T1 to T3 mark transitional stages between those levels, and U marks a resource whose maturity is unrated because it has not yet been measured. The maturity label on this record and its implementation status are two different facts and both are stated: a design can be specified (E3) while its implementation status is still proposed, and a prototype can be tested without being verified or deployed.

When it applies: This record applies whenever someone must do, check, or teach Dependency Registration inside the Universal Creation and Construction Database: the builder follows the method, the checker runs the acceptance tests, and the teacher walks the teaching ladder from the simple description to the full specification. This database is linked to, never a rebuild of, the Foundation program's staged universal-construction-generator pair and its working Python prototype, the staged meta-generator pair, and the staged automated-testing-generator pair, all package-only, and to the Digital Singularity AI pair, package-only, which is the construction engine the paste's section 10 points at. It also links the live trade databases construction-systems, construction-tools, and construction-materials, which cover building trades, not the methods of generating new systems that this database records.

Its risks are written down before work starts: what can go wrong, what the failure would look like, and what safe state the work falls back to, so a failure is a handled event, not a surprise. Scope: The scope covers the whole creation path and nothing outside it. Inside the scope are requirements, scope and boundary setting, component catalogues, interface contracts, data models, build sequences, prototypes, test harnesses, verification checklists, documentation, versioning, reuse libraries, templates, workflows, human review gates, rollback plans, resource budgets, dependency registration, integration tests, acceptance criteria, deployment checklists, maintenance handover, improvement loops, and provenance records. Outside the scope are the contents of the systems being created, which belong to their own subject databases, and the operation of finished systems, which belongs to their own records. This database records how to create; it does not duplicate what was created. Requirements: Every creation method in this database must meet eight requirements. First, it must name its inputs exactly, so a builder knows what to gather before starting. Second, it must name its outputs exactly, so a checker knows what to look for when the step ends. Third, it must be executable: a real program or a real procedure that runs, not a description of one. Fourth, it must carry acceptance tests with numbers, so pass and fail are decided by measurement, not by opinion. Fifth, it must preserve evidence: every check leaves a record with a date and a result. Sixth, it must support rollback, so a failed step returns the work to a known good state. Seventh, it must be teachable, written in plain words with every technical term defined. Eighth, it must state its maturity and its implementation status plainly, so no reader mistakes a proposal for a finished system. Interfaces: A creation method connects to the rest of the network through four interfaces. The input interface accepts requirements, prior records, and source material, each with an identifier and a provenance note. The catalog interface registers the method in the Master AI registry, with its unique identifier, its version, its dependencies, and its implementation status. The generator interface exposes the method as a seeded generator that can produce further instances on demand, up to one billion scales per file, generated, not stored. The evidence interface writes every check result to the verification record, where the Master AI and the specialist artificial intelligences can read it. All four interfaces use plain text and standard formats, so any part of the network can read them without special tools. Every resource in this program moves through the universal resource lifecycle of the paste's section 7, in order: 1. Discover, identifying existing resources and determining whether the resource already exists. 2. Read and understand, inspecting the actual records, files, code, specifications, and dependencies. 3. Specify, defining what the resource must do and how it will connect to other resources. 4. Build, producing the actual implementation. 5. Connect, registering the resource with the Master AI and the relevant specialist artificial intelligences. 6. Test, running functional, integration, security, and reliability tests. 7. Validate, comparing actual results against requirements and preserving the evidence. 8. Expand, adding records, capabilities, generators, and new resource families. The implementation status of this record is stated plainly in its maturity line, using the paste's section 9 words: theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, or deployed, and nothing is called deployed until it has actually been deployed and checked live. The Boundless Generator standard of the paste's section 6 applies to this record: a generator must produce a complete deliverable for its category, not a text description of one. A database generator produces a schema, records, search, an application programming interface, and documentation. A website generator produces pages, navigation, functionality, and a responsive interface. A software generator produces source code, configuration, dependencies, and tests. An artificial-intelligence generator produces an architecture, model connections, tools, and an evaluation. A meta-generator must create and test new generators, not merely describe how one might be created. An ecosystem generator produces connected resources, interfaces, and a dependency registry. Every generated deliverable is tested by execution before it is counted, and every output carries its seed, its version, and its implementation status.

Results reported: The result of this record is a complete specification for Maintenance Handover that a builder can follow and a checker can test. Acceptance for a record in this database is decided by execution and by counting. The demo must run and its output must match the recorded expected output byte for byte, including the line that reads Deterministic: MATCH. The program must run and agree with the demo. The record must carry at least two cited sources, each a real web address verified as reachable at validation time, with a retrieval date. The prose must reach at least eight hundred words, counted over the prose fields and the body. The record must carry a maturity label and an implementation status, the lifecycle steps, the generator standard, and the God-Level and Boundless distinctions in plain words. A record that fails any of these is not accepted, and the failure is recorded, not hidden. Its acceptance tests are the tests in the acceptance paragraph: the demo must run byte-identical, the program must agree, sources must be verified, words must reach eight hundred, and maturity and status must be stated. Maturity: E3. Implementation status: proposed, on the paste's section 9 scale of theoretical, proposed, generated, implemented, tested, verified, and deployed. Maturity is labeled on the network scale: E1 means an early idea, E2 a drafted proposal, E3 a specified design, E4 a built prototype, E5 a tested prototype, E6 a verified system, E7 a deployed system in live use; T1 to T3 mark transitional stages between those levels, and U marks a resource whose maturity is unrated because it has not yet been measured. The maturity label on this record and its implementation status are two different facts and both are stated: a design can be specified (E3) while its implementation status is still proposed, and a prototype can be tested without being verified or deployed.

When it applies: This record applies whenever someone must do, check, or teach Maintenance Handover inside the Universal Creation and Construction Database: the builder follows the method, the checker runs the acceptance tests, and the teacher walks the teaching ladder from the simple description to the full specification. This database is linked to, never a rebuild of, the Foundation program's staged universal-construction-generator pair and its working Python prototype, the staged meta-generator pair, and the staged automated-testing-generator pair, all package-only, and to the Digital Singularity AI pair, package-only, which is the construction engine the paste's section 10 points at. It also links the live trade databases construction-systems, construction-tools, and construction-materials, which cover building trades, not the methods of generating new systems that this database records.

Universal Creation and Construction: Improvement Loops

JAH-UCAC-000023 · Improvement Loops

Problem: The problem this record addresses is the absence of a complete, checkable specification for Improvement Loops inside the Universal Creation and Construction Database. Without such a record, builders must guess at the method, checkers have nothing definite to test against, and the same work is repeated badly in many places. The gap is real because the cross-check verified it against the live manifest by exact match, not by guessing: the slugs and identifier prefixes were confirmed free before any record was written. The purpose of this database is to record, in full, the methods by which a new system is created from an idea to a working, tested deliverable. A system here means any complete working thing the network can build: a database, a website, a program, an artificial intelligence, a generator, or a connected ecosystem of those. Creation without recorded method is luck; creation with recorded method is repeatable, teachable, and checkable. Every record in this database therefore answers the same question for its own step: what must be true before the step starts, what the step produces, how the product is checked, and where the evidence is kept, so that any builder following the record can produce the same kind of result and any checker can verify it.

Method: This record specifies Improvement Loops as one working part of the Universal Creation and Construction Database. Its working angle is handover. 7. AI

The AI of this database is JAH Universal Creation Librarian. It explains this database in plain, conversational language, answers questions about any of the 24 records, and can create an AI assistant exactly like itself. It answers from this database's own data only, and says plainly when a question goes beyond it.

  • ai-thing.json — the per-database AI spec (the database's own AI, JAH Universal Creation Librarian).
  • ai-discussion.json — the discussion AI spec, built to discuss Universal Creation and Construction Database with a visitor, record by record.

Both are free downloads, free forever, no sign-in.

8. Demo

Every record in this database carries a working demo program and a boundless generator. The demo for this website is demo.js — run it with node demo.js. The run output is saved in demo-test.txt and shown below, exactly as executed — this demo actually ran.
